L2305: The following postmarketing requirements (PMRs) are recommended by the Division of Pediatrics and Maternal Health (DPMH) and describe a pregnancy exposure registry study, complementary pregnancy study, and lactation study.
L2306: 1. Collect global data from prospective pregnancy exposure registry/registries, preferably disease-based multiproduct pregnancy registry/registries, using a registry-based cohort study design to compare the maternal, fetal, and infant outcomes of women exposed to Journavx during pregnancy with unexposed comparator population(s). Align the U.S. study protocol with protocol(s) outside the United States to reach the target sample size. The registry will identify and record pregnancy complications, spontaneous abortion, stillbirths, neonatal deaths, major and minor congenital malformations, pregnancy terminations, preterm births, small-for-gestational-age births, and any other adverse outcomes, including postnatal growth and development. These outcomes should be assessed throughout pregnancy. Infant
L2307: outcomes, including effects on postnatal growth and development, will be assessed through at least the first year of life.
L2308: Draft protocol submission: 07/2025
L2309: Final protocol submission: 01/2026
L2310: Interim/Other: 01/2027, 01/2028, 01/2029, 01/2030, 01/2031, 01/2032, 01/2033, 01/2034, 01/2035
L2311: Study completion: 01/2036
L2312: Final report submission: 07/2036
L2313: 2. Conduct a retrospective pregnancy cohort study using claims or electronic health record data with medical chart validation that is adequately powered to assess spontaneous abortions, stillbirths, neonatal deaths, major congenital malformations, preterm births, and small-for-gestational-age births in individuals exposed to Journavx during pregnancy compared to appropriate comparator population(s).
L2314: Draft protocol submission: 07/2025
L2315: Final protocol submission: 01/2026
L2316: Interim/Other: 01/2028, 01/2030, 01/2032, 01/2034
L2317: Study completion: 01/2036
L2318: Final report submission: 07/2036
L2319: 3. Perform a milk-only lactation study in lactating women who have received Journavx to measure concentrations of suzetrigine in breast milk using a validated assay. Assess the effects on the breastfed infant, if available, based on study population.

L2386: Figure 1 displays two line graphs comparing Placebo (gray line) and VX-548 (blue line) groups over time (0 to 48 hours). The left graph shows Mean NPRS (SE) on the y-axis (0 to 10). Both groups start around 6.5 and decrease, with VX-548 consistently lower than Placebo. The right graph shows Mean Pain Intensity Difference (SE) on the y-axis (-4.5 to 1.0). Both groups start around 0.5 and decrease, with VX-548 consistently lower than Placebo, indicating greater pain reduction.
L2387: Figure 2 displays two line graphs comparing Placebo (gray line) and VX-548 (blue line) groups over time (hours) for Study 105. The left graph shows Mean NPRS (SE) on the y-axis (0 to 10). The right graph shows Mean Pain Intensity Difference (SE) on the y-axis (-4.5 to 1.0). Both graphs show that the VX-548 group consistently exhibits lower mean scores/differences compared to the Placebo group across all evaluated time points, indicating superior efficacy.
L2388: Figure 3 displays two line graphs comparing the proportion of subjects using rescue medication (Y-axis, %) across three treatment groups (X-axis, Hours: 6, 12, 24, 48) for Studies 104 and 105.
Trial 104 (Left Graph): The HB/APAP group (red line) shows the lowest proportion of subjects using rescue medication, starting around 55% at 6 hours and rising to about 80% at 48 hours. The Placebo (green line) and SUZ (blue line) groups show significantly higher proportions, starting around 70% and 65% respectively at 6 hours, and rising to about 85% and 88% respectively at 48 hours. The Placebo and SUZ groups show similar trends and proportions throughout the study period.
Trial 105 (Right Graph): The HB/APAP group (red line) starts around 72% at 6 hours and rises to about 82% at 48 hours. The Placebo (green line) and SUZ (blue line) groups start around 82% and 65% respectively at 6 hours, and rise to about 88% and 82% respectively at 48 hours. The Placebo group consistently shows the highest proportion of subjects using rescue medication, followed by SUZ, while HB/APAP shows the lowest proportion.
Legend for both graphs:
HB/APAP (Red line)
Placebo (Green line)
SUZ (Blue line)
L2389: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis for subjects in pooled Studies 104 and 105. The plot is divided into four quadrants by dashed lines at 2.0 xULN (Y-axis) and 3.0 xULN (X-axis). The top left quadrant is labeled 'Cholestasis'. The top right quadrant is labeled 'Potential Hy's Law'. The bottom right quadrant is labeled 'Temple's Corollary'. Data points are categorized by treatment: SUZ (blue dots), HB/APAP (red squares), and Placebo (open circles). Most data points cluster near the origin, indicating low liver injury. A few outliers are visible, particularly in the Temple's Corollary quadrant.

A point labeled 'Temple's Corollary' is located around X=12, Y=1.2. The legend indicates 'SUZ' (Suzetrigine) is represented by blue dots.

L2393: Figure 8 illustrates the proposed biotransformation pathway of Suzetrigine (SUZ, M3, VRT-1875424). The pathway shows various metabolic steps leading to metabolites M1 through M18.
The starting compound (M3) undergoes:
O-demethylation to M2.
Oxidation to M9.
Glucuronidation to M1 and M15.
Sulfation to M10.
Reaction with +O, -F, +SG to M7.
M2 undergoes:
Reaction with +O, -F, +SG to M8.
Pyridine N-oxidation to M6 (VRT-1875420).
M8 undergoes:
Reaction with +O, -F, +SG to M16.
O-demethylation to M17.
M6 undergoes:
O-demethylation to M12 (VRT-1875671).
M12 undergoes:
Glucuronidation to M18.
M9 undergoes:
Oxidation to M11.
Oxidation to M14.
M11 undergoes:
Reaction with +O to M17.
M14 undergoes:
Reaction with +O to M17.
M17 undergoes:
Reaction with +O and +SG to M17.
L2394: Figure 9 illustrates the structural components of Suzetrigine (SUZ). The molecule is composed of three main moieties:
THF Core (Tetrahydrofuran ring system)
Phenoxy ring (Benzofuran derivative ring system)
Pyridine Headgroup (Pyridine ring attached to the THF core)
L2395: Chemical structures illustrating the biotransformation pathway of VRT-1875424 (M3-SUZ) into metabolites M1-424 and M2-424.
VRT-1875424 (Starting Compound): A molecule featuring a cyclopropyl ring substituted with \text{CF}_3, an ethyl group, and a carbonyl group attached to a benzofuranone ring system (containing a hydroxyl group and two fluorine atoms). The carbonyl group is also attached to a pyridine ring substituted with an \text{NH}_2 group.
M1-424 (Top Product): Formed by oxidation (+O) of the hydroxyl group on the benzofuranone ring. The structure is identical to VRT-1875424, except for the oxidized hydroxyl group. It is labeled +Glu. Description: All species - major in rat. Previously observed as VRT-1737548 M1.
M2-424 (Bottom Product): Formed by oxidation (+O) of the hydroxyl group on the benzofuranone ring. The structure is identical to VRT-1875424, except for the oxidized hydroxyl group. It is labeled +Glu. Description: All species - major in dog, cyno and human. Previously observed as VRT-1737548 M4.
L2396: Figure 15 displays two line graphs showing Mean (+SD) Plasma Trough-Concentration-Time Profiles for SUZ (left) and M6-SUZ (right) across 14 days of multiple doses. The Y-axis represents Mean Concentration (\mug/mL) and the X-axis represents Day. Multiple dose cohorts are plotted: 5 mg qd, 15 mg qd, 45 mg qd, 65 mg qd, 90 mg qd, 115/57.5 mg q12h, and 178 mg qd. For both SUZ and M6-SUZ, trough concentrations generally increase over the first 10 days, followed by a plateau or slight decrease, indicating potential autoinduction. Higher doses (90 mg qd and 178 mg qd) show higher trough concentrations compared to lower doses (5 mg qd and 15 mg qd).

L2403: Flowchart illustrating the Study 101 design. The timeline starts with Screening Period start (Day -28), CRU admission; Bunionectionomy (Morning of Day -1), and Popliteal block removal (Morning of Day 1). The time between Popliteal block removal and Request study drug for pain relief (NPRS/VRS); Randomization is <=9 hours. The Treatment Period follows, consisting of 5 groups: VX-548 (high), VX-548 (mid), VX-548 (low), HB/APAP, and Placebo. The treatment period ends with 48-hour assessments; CRU discharge (Day 3), followed by a Safety Follow-up Visit 14 +/- 2 days after last dose.
L2404: Figure 23 illustrates the Study Design for Study 102, a clinical trial comparing VX-548 (high and low doses) against HB/APAP and Placebo in patients undergoing abdominoplasty. The timeline spans from Day -28 (Screening Period start) to 14 \pm 2 days after the last dose.
Screening Period: Starts on Day -28.
CRU Admission/Abdominoplasty: Occurs on Morning of Day 1, approximately 54 hours after the Screening Period start.
Request study drug for pain relief (VRS & NPRS) Randomization: Occurs immediately following CRU admission/abdominoplasty.
Treatment Period: Begins on Day 1 and continues until Day 3. During this period, subjects are randomized to receive VX-548 (high), VX-548 (low), HB/APAP, or Placebo.
48-hour assessments; CRU discharge: Occurs on Day 3.
Safety Follow-up Visit: Occurs 14 \pm 2 days after the last dose.
L2405: Diagram illustrating the Final Population PK Model of SUZ/M6-SUZ. The model is divided into two main pathways: SUZ (Parent) and M6-SUZ (Metabolite).
SUZ (Parent) Pathway:
Dose input splits based on conditions: "If abdominoplasty and TIME < MTIME" or "Not abdominoplasty, or abdominoplasty and TIME > MTIME".
Both paths lead to (1) Depot.
From (1) Depot, SUZ enters (2) Central (V_{2P}/F_1) via absorption rate K_{AD} (if abdominoplasty) or K_A (otherwise).
(2) Central (V_{2P}/F_1) is connected to (3) Peripheral (V_{3P}/F_1) via intercompartmental transport Q_{3P}.
(2) Central (V_{2P}/F_1) is connected to (6) AENZ via apparent clearance CLP/F_1 * (1 - FR) * AENZ.
(2) Central (V_{2P}/F_1) is connected to (4) Central (V_{2M}/F_1) via apparent clearance CLP/F_1 * FR * AENZ.
M6-SUZ (Metabolite) Pathway:
(4) Central (V_{2M}/F_1) is connected to (5) Peripheral (V_{3M}/F_1) via intercompartmental transport Q_{3M}.
(4) Central (V_{2M}/F_1) is connected to (6) AENZ via apparent clearance CLM * AENZ.
AENZ Pathway:
(6) AENZ is connected to (2) Central (V_{2P}/F_1) via induced enzyme formation and degradation rate K_{ENZ} * \left(1 + \frac{AMAX * C_{PAHILL}}{AC_{50AHILL} + C_{PAHILL}}\right).
(6) AENZ is connected to (4) Central (V_{2M}/F_1) via induced enzyme formation and degradation rate K_{ENZ}.

A vertical dashed line indicates the "Coadministered w/Fluconazole" level around 60 \text{nmol}\cdot\text{hr}/\text{mL}.
Right Panel: Dose-relationship in Abdominoplasty

Y-axis: LS mean difference from placebo of SPID48 (\pm SE), ranging from -20 to 60.
Data points (dose regimens): 60 mg/30 mg q12h (green circle) and 100 mg/50 mg q12h (red circle).

L2425: Figure 44 displays three simulated pharmacokinetic (PK) profiles over 5 days for subjects who received a regular dose and missed one dose. The legend indicates the black line represents the Regular dose and the orange line represents the Missed 1 dose.
SUZ Concentration (left panel): The concentration ranges from 0.0 to 1.2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. Both profiles show similar trough and peak concentrations, indicating minimal impact on SUZ levels after missing one dose.
M6-SUZ Concentration (middle panel): The concentration ranges from 0.0 to 2.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The Regular dose profile shows a steady increase in concentration over time, reaching approximately 1.8 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} by day 5. The Missed 1 dose profile shows a lower concentration, reaching approximately 1.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} by day 5, indicating a significant reduction in M6-SUZ levels.
Combined SUZ and M6-SUZ Concentration (right panel): The concentration ranges from 0.0 to 4.0 \text{nmol}/\text{mL}. Both profiles show similar trough and peak concentrations, indicating minimal impact on the combined concentration after missing one dose.
L2426: Figure 45 displays three simulated pharmacokinetic (PK) profiles over 5 days for subjects who received a regular dose and missed two doses. The legend indicates the black line represents the Regular dose and the orange line represents the Missed 2 doses.
SUZ Concentration (left panel): The concentration ranges from 0.0 to 1.2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. Both profiles show similar trough and peak concentrations, indicating minimal impact on SUZ levels after missing two doses.
M6-SUZ Concentration (middle panel): The concentration ranges from 0.0 to 2.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The Regular dose profile shows a steady increase in concentration over time, reaching approximately 1.8 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} by day 5. The Missed 2 doses profile shows a significantly lower concentration, reaching approximately 1.0 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} by day 5, indicating a substantial reduction in M6-SUZ levels.
Combined SUZ and M6-SUZ Concentration (right panel): The concentration ranges from 0.0 to 4.0 \text{nmol}/\text{mL}. Both profiles show similar trough and peak concentrations, indicating minimal impact on the combined concentration after missing two doses.

L2429: Figure 48 displays four concentration-time profiles (log scale) for VX-548 (SUZ) and M6-548 (M6-SUZ) after a single 20-mg dose in healthy subjects, comparing administration without and with Itraconazole. The Y-axis represents Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.001 to 1000). The X-axis represents Time (hours) (0 to 192).
VX-548 only: Shows rapid clearance of SUZ. Observed data (solid circles) and simulated mean (solid line) decrease significantly over time, falling below the 5th percentile (shaded area) by 192 hours.
VX-548 with Itraconazole: Shows sustained high concentrations of SUZ. Observed data and simulated mean remain above the 95th percentile (shaded area) throughout the 192 hours.
M6-548 only: Shows sustained high concentrations of M6-SUZ. Observed data and simulated mean remain above the 95th percentile (shaded area) throughout the 192 hours.
M6-548 with Itraconazole: Shows sustained high concentrations of M6-SUZ. Observed data and simulated mean remain above the 95th percentile (shaded area) throughout the 192 hours.
L2430: Figure 49 displays four concentration-time profiles (log scale) for VX-548 (SUZ) and M6-548 (M6-SUZ) after a single 100-mg dose in healthy subjects, comparing conditions without and with Rifampin. The Y-axis represents Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.001 to 10000), and the X-axis represents Time (hours) (0 to 192).
The top row shows profiles for VX-548 (SUZ):
VX-548 only: Observed data (solid circles) and simulated mean (solid line) show a rapid decline in concentration, reaching approximately 100 ng/mL by 48 hours and stabilizing around 10 ng/mL by 192 hours. The shaded area represents the 5th to 95th percentile of the virtual population.
VX-548 with Rifampin: Observed data and simulated mean show a significantly faster clearance, with concentration dropping below 1 ng/mL by 48 hours and remaining low thereafter.
The bottom row shows profiles for M6-548 (M6-SUZ):
M6-548 only: Observed data and simulated mean show a rapid decline in concentration, reaching approximately 100 ng/mL by 48 hours and stabilizing around 10 ng/mL by 192 hours.
M6-548 with Rifampin: Observed data and simulated mean show a significantly faster clearance, with concentration dropping below 1 ng/mL by 48 hours and remaining low thereafter.

Panel A: Baseline Dosing Regimen

Bottom Graph (M6-SUZ Concentration): Shows M6-SUZ concentration (ng/mL) over time (h). The concentration rises sharply after the initial dose and then exhibits a sustained, high-amplitude oscillatory profile. The legend indicates two lines: CSys (dotted line) and CSys+interaction (solid line). The CSys+interaction line is consistently higher than the CSys line.

L2435: Figure 54: Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot. The plot shows Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis (0.0 to 2.0) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis (0 to 9). The plot is divided into four quadrants: Cholestasis (Top Left), Potential Hy's Law (Top Right), and Temple's Corollary (Bottom Right). The legend indicates: SUZ (blue diamond), HB/APAP (red square), Placebo (open circle).
